 * Trace tab surface. The local --ns-trace-* scale is aliased to the V3 semantic
 * tokens (nodeslide V3.css, which wins the cascade) so the trace surface inherits
 * light/dark theme flips for free. The soft companions are derived via color-mix.
 * Visual pattern (rail spine, typed badges, color-handoff) is adapted from
 * Agent Prism by Evil Martians (MIT) — referenced, not imported.

  }
}

}

/*
 * trust-surfaces clause 2: `ready` used to share this rule with `accepted` and `completed`,
 * so a proposal that was merely READY TO REVIEW wore the exact success green of one a human
 * had already accepted — an undecided thing painted in the colour of one outcome. `ready`
 * gets the attention token instead: it has arrived and it is waiting, which is what it is.
 */
.ns-status-dot--ready {
  background: var(--ns-accent);
}
.ns-status-dot--stale,
.ns-status-dot--failed {
  background: var(--ns-danger);
}

.ns-status-dot--working,
.ns-status-dot--validating {

}

  display: grid;
  grid-template-rows: 68px 40px minmax(0, 1fr);
  /* Definite height (not just max-height): a content-sized dialog inside an
     align-items:center backdrop leaves no free space, so the minmax(0,1fr) body
     row collapses to 0 and the deck list/form gets clipped. A bounded height gives
     the body row space to fill and scroll internally. */
  height: min(760px, calc(100dvh - 48px));
  overflow: hidden;
  /* inset:0 + margin:auto centers the box whether the native <dialog> resolves to
     position:absolute (UA) — where flex centering does not apply — or to the
     authored position:relative in-flow flex child. Robust in both cases. */
  inset: 0;
  margin: auto;
  padding: 0;
  position: relative;
  width: min(760px, 100%);
}

/* trust-surfaces clause 2, and the reason this rule reads differently from the one it was
 * ported from.
 *
 * Parity drew this state on `--ns-positive` — the success colour — because the local action
 * succeeded. But the sentence it paints is "the deck is unchanged until you accept it": the
 * change is UNDECIDED, and it is undecided by design, because this lab deliberately cannot
 * apply anything. Green says the opposite of the words, and colour is read before copy. The
 * state is now drawn as unresolved: neutral surface, a dashed edge that does not close, and
 * `data-decision="undecided"` on the same node so an agent reads the same posture a person
 * sees. Same defect the AgentThread proposal card carried, same fix. */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-openui-status.is-proposed,
.nodeslide-studio .ns-openui-status[data-decision="undecided"] {
  background: var(--ns-raised);
  border: 1px dashed var(--ns-line-f);
  color: var(--ns-ink2);
}

/* An error is a failure, not a decision, so it keeps its colour and gets no decision state. */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-openui-status.is-error {
  background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--ns-danger) 9%, transparent);
  color: var(--ns-danger);
}

/*
 * Binding coverage. How many claims the deck makes, how many carry a source, and
 * how many do not — three values at one size, label muted and value full
 * contrast, so nothing is made smaller to say "this matters less".
 * A claim with no source is usually mid-work, so the unsourced control is dashed
 * and accent-bordered, never an alarm colour, and it is inert while the count is
 * zero rather than inviting a click into an empty list.
 */
.ns-evidence-coverage {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 6px;
  margin-top: 9px;
}

.ns-evidence-coverage > span,
.ns-evidence-unsourced-toggle {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 6px;
  min-height: 26px;
  padding: 3px 8px;
  border: 1px solid var(--ns-border, #ddd6cd);
  border-radius: 8px;
  background: transparent;
  font: inherit;
}

.ns-evidence-coverage > span > span,
.ns-evidence-unsourced-toggle > span {
  color: var(--ns-muted, #6d665f);
}

.ns-evidence-coverage strong {
  color: var(--ns-ink, #2b2620);
  font-weight: 650;
}

.ns-evidence-unsourced-toggle:enabled {
  border-style: dashed;
  border-color: var(--ns-accent, #b7803a);
  cursor: pointer;
}

.ns-evidence-unsourced-toggle:disabled {
  cursor: default;

/* Two authoring tabs, then five review tabs.
 *
 * The seven were presented as peers, so the whole set was priced at the strip before any panel
 * loaded. The divider states the real structure: AI and Design are what you use while making a
 * slide; Comments, Versions, Evidence, JSON and Trace are what you open to check work that already
 * exists. Nothing is hidden — see the note in InspectorPanel.tsx for why the reduction Figma makes
 * is wrong for this product specifically.
 *
 * A hairline and a colour step, not a heading. The strip is 31px tall and holds seven labels in a
 * 340px rail; anything louder would cost more attention than the grouping saves.
 */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-tabs__divider {
  align-self: center;
  background: var(--ns-line-b);
  flex: none;
  height: 11px;
  width: 1px;
}

/* Review tabs sit back until they are the thing you want.
 *
 * `:not(.is-active)` rather than relying on source order. This rule sits AFTER the .is-active rule
 * at equal specificity, so ordering alone would have made the selected Evidence or Trace tab render
 * faint — the same cascade mistake that made the first type-scale attempt do nothing. Excluding the
 * active tab in the selector is true regardless of where either rule lands.
 */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-tabs button.is-review:not(.is-active) {
  color: var(--ns-faint);
}

.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-content {
  color: var(--ns-ink);
  overflow: hidden;
}

  overflow: hidden;

/* The composer belongs at the bottom edge, the way Cursor and every chat surface put it — you type
   at the bottom and the conversation grows above. It was `order: 1`, which pinned the input to the
   TOP of its own container and pushed the suggestions, policy line and disclosure underneath it, so
   the thing you actually type into had 64px of chips sitting below it.
   High numbers rather than a re-shuffle: the other rows keep their relative order (3, 4, 5) and only
   the input and its token chips move to the end. */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-suggested-actions {
  order: 3;
}

.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-composer > .ns-composer-tokens {
  order: 19;
}

.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-composer-field {
  flex: none;
  order: 20;
}

/* ============================================================
 * Agentic conversation thread (Cursor / v0 style)
 * The AI tab reads as a real agent chat: user asks as bubbles on
 * the right, agent replies as soft cards with an inline work rail,
 * and the proposal as an inline patch card. Appended last so it
 * wins the cascade over the earlier minimal turn rules.
 * ============================================================ */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-review-scroll {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 13px;
  padding: 14px 12px 6px;
}

.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-chat-turn {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: 22px minmax(0, 1fr);
  gap: 8px;
  align-items: start;
  margin: 0;
}
.nodeslide-studio .ns-ai-v3-chat-turn.is-user {

 * Landing creation wait (G4)
 * Honest, elapsed-time-driven staged narrative while a creation
 * is in flight. No fake percent bars: the server does not stream
 * progress, so copy advances on wall-clock stages plus a visible
 * elapsed timer.

  color: var(--ns-faint);
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  margin-left: auto;
}

/* Inspector type scale — four steps, and it has to live HERE to work.
 *
 * The Design tab was measured rendering eight distinct sizes between 8px and 19px inside a 344px
 * column. Eight sizes is not a hierarchy; it is what accumulates when every addition brings its own
 * font-size. Linear's Properties panel renders label and value at ONE size and distinguishes the
 * label by colour — that is the rule adopted here.
 *
 * The first attempt put this block at line ~1620 of nodeslide.css, with a comment claiming it would
 * win "on source order". It did not, and a production sweep proved it: the Design tab still reported
 * eight sizes afterwards. Two things beat it, both later in the cascade at equal specificity —
 * the `--ns-chrome-min-font` clamp lists at nodeslide.css:1963 and :3647, and this entire file,
 * which NodeSlideStudio.tsx imports after nodeslide.css. Being "after the section rules" is not the
 * same as being last.
 *
 * So it lives at the end of the last-loaded stylesheet. That is the honest place for a rule whose
 * whole job is to be the final word on inspector text size.
 */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-scroll :is(h2, h3) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-title);
  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
}
.nodeslide-studio
  .ns-inspector-scroll
  :is(p, label, li, td, th, input, select, textarea, button, strong) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-body);
}
/* `small` is the caption step, not a licence to go under the floor. */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-scroll small {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-caption);
}
/* Tags are uppercase and letter-spaced, so they read larger than their size. */
.nodeslide-studio
  .ns-inspector-scroll
  :is(.ns-eyebrow, .ns-kind-pill, .ns-count-pill, .ns-route-pill) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-tag);
}
/* Anything still unclaimed inside the panel gets the floor rather than whatever it inherited.
 * This is the line that removes the 8px and 9px stragglers the sweep found. */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-scroll span:not([class]) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-tag);
}

/* The panel HEADER, which is not inside .ns-inspector-scroll.
 *
 * A production sweep after the first fix reported six sizes where the harness had shown four. The
 * harness was not wrong — it only contained the markup written into it, and the panel header was not
 * in it. Scoping to `.ns-inspector-scroll` had left the header untouched by design, and the sweep
 * measures the whole panel.
 *
 * The survivors, named by probing production rather than guessed: `.ns-eyebrow` at 10px, and the
 * context chips `.is-slide` and `.is-empty` at 9px. Bringing them to the tag step is what takes the
 * panel to four sizes end to end.
 */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector :is(.ns-eyebrow, .ns-inspector-context-chips span) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-tag);
}

/* The binding-coverage row, which reads `Claims 43 · Bound 43 · Unsourced 0`.
 *
 * Its labels are spans and its values are `strong`, so the two rules above split
 * the row across the tag step and the body step: measured on a local build
 * against production data, `Claims` came out 11px and `43` came out 13px. A row
 * whose label is smaller than its value is the exact thing this block exists to
 * stop, so both halves are pinned to the body step and the label is separated by
 * colour instead. Last in the last-loaded stylesheet, for the reason above.
 */
.nodeslide-studio .ns-inspector-scroll .ns-evidence-coverage :is(span, strong) {
  font-size: var(--ns-font-body);
}
